Method and marks. [read] All rows below come from the 12 part files; I added no incident and no fact of my own.
Each part row was parsed by script (number, then six fields separated by " | "). Duplicates were found by a script that
proposed pairs (same date within 1-3 days plus shared words, numbers, hashes or primary paths) and by me reading each
proposed pair; I merged only where both rows describe the same event. [inferred] Two rows of one event that share no
words or paths could have been missed, so the merged count is a ceiling on distinct events, not an exact count.
Where parts disagree on wording, the row kept is the one with a [read] primary with a line number and a recorded cost,
and the others are named at the end of the primary field as "(also: part#row primary)". One part row (librarian_nov 81)
had a literal " | " inside its event text; I wrote " / " there so the row stays one line of six fields.
Row format: id | date | machine | what happened | what it cost | who or what caught it | primary (also: ...).
Sorted by date of the kept row (a range sorts by its start; a month-only start sorts first in its month); no ranking, no grouping.
Merges I was unsure of (kept as one row, other views in "also"): the 11-13 idle-lock episode (the lock held, the librarian not
checking the child); the D625 landing (one row for the render_proof reds; later steps of that landing are separate rows);
the extra-bank drag winding (the fault, the keeper catch and the two wrong inferred causes are merged except pane E N2 reading).
Related rows kept separate on purpose: the 7,000-wide reader grep vs the orphaned 16,000-wide grep at the gate; each 403 burst;
each "No response requested" reply (11-05, 11-07); the lock stalls of 11-12 and 11-13.

## What I did not open

- No primary source was opened. I read only the 12 part files (by script and, for the proposed duplicate pairs, by eye) and the plan file.
- Every id, count and merge here is the product of that pass over the part files. No transcript, board, data jsonl, hand-back, journal or git history was read.
- Duplicate pairs with low word overlap, dates more than 3 days apart, or different wording of the same event may remain as separate rows.
- Rows of the keeper-catches and third-place parts that quote him were not re-checked against his words.
- I did not check any part file claim marked [read], [cited], [subject] or [inferred]; each mark is carried as the part wrote it.


## Second reader (Sonnet research agent, 2026-11-13)

Sample [read: node script]: 1123 rows counted; every 19th row starting at row 10 (L0010, L0029, ... L1112) gives 59 rows;
plus the 15 rows with the largest stated cost (a regex over the cost field, tokens converted to thousands, hours to
minutes; units differ, so this is a rough top 15). Four of the 15 are already in the 59, so 70 distinct rows were opened.
Each primary was opened at the cited path:line in whole lines. Briefs short names resolved by me: sb.md =
briefs/t180_second_bank.md, dz2.md = briefs/dreamzone2_the_game.md, the_room.md = briefs/lighthouse_the_room.md.
Commit rows ("[subject]") were read with git show -s. Counts were re-derived by node over the jsonl files where the row
quoted them: carrier-drift 4117/2050/68/1982 reproduce; precompact 1420/1090/671 reproduce; sessionstart 471 reproduces;
reply-slot 47 blocked (43+4) and 54 would-block before Oct 12 reproduce; sources-gate warns are 2468 not 2467 (714 not 713);
deny 0 in the window reproduces.

as 83.37 M.

Duplicates (a path-key scan over all 1123 rows: same primary path and line, then read by me): three rows are the same event
as another row. L0255 repeats L0239; L0300 repeats L0294; L0299 repeats L0290 (and names the chair where the trace says the
librarian). All three marked REMOVE in place. [inferred] The scan only meets pairs that cite the same line, so more
duplicates with different citations may remain; none of the 70 sampled rows had a twin by a word-overlap check.

Tally of problems: of the systematic 59, 6 rows have a problem (4 not incidents, 2 small wrong figures), 10.2%; strict
removal share 4 of 59, 6.8%. Of the 11 extra large-cost rows, 0. The weak class is the commit-subject rows: 9 sampled rows
rest on a subject line only (L0390 L0409 L0447 L0466 L0485 L0618 L0903 L0960 L1112) and 2 of them are not incidents.

Estimate [inferred, from a small sample, wide band]: about 7% of the ledger (roughly 75 of 1123 rows; plausible range
2% to 16%) should be removed as not an incident, mostly finished-work commit rows and leads that call themselves leads;
a further 3% (roughly 35 rows) carry a small wrong figure; plus about 0.3% (3 rows) found as duplicates and an unknown
few more. So roughly 10% of the ledger is unreliable as written. Large-cost rows and rows with a [read] primary at
a line held up best (0 problems in 11 and in the rows with jsonl or hand-back primaries); scorers should discount the
commit-subject rows (about 120 to 165 rows of the ledger, estimated 20% bad) and the AGG rows built on counts.
The dwell, token and hours figures in the big rows are real; they are not a split by cause (L0076 says so itself).
